Top 10 under 22%: 34 in the table, 36 in the raw data (JUSTICE 21.99% and COALMAS 21.96% were printed as 22.0); snipers 7%+: 47 / 46 (ZBRA 6.99%). VCAT's $29,826 is a second Jupiter reading taken 0.85 s after the check, not the reading the rule used. Every other figure it gave reproduces exactly (29 and 11 doublers; its two rules 19 buys +68.26% and 11 buys +111.91%; the fee bands; 10-minute t20 against t25: better 29, worse 7, tied 31, +4.46 points).
  - **The search (plan fixed first): 23 factors, both directions, percentile grid + his own lines, at least 10 buys kept and 10 left out.** 5-minute: 17,568 two-factor + 205,652 three-factor rules (170,280 distinct kept sets). 10-minute: 14,260 + 125,052 (94,377 distinct). Chance test: results shuffled between buys 300 times and the whole search repeated.
  - **5-minute, best rule found against best rule chance finds:** t25 mean +148.7% against a chance median of +129.1% (95% +147.7%; p 0.043) · 2× rate edge +69 points against +59 (p 0.08) · capped t25 edge p 0.15 · t40 mean +135.7% against +149.0% by chance (p 0.77) · capped t40 p 0.35 · severe-loss edge p 0.33. One ranking of six under 0.05, none after allowing for six. **A search this size finds a ten-buy rule averaging about +130% on shuffled results.** 10-minute: every ranking p 0.13–0.64. Wallet cohorts (phishing / bots / fresh; 48 and 38 buys): every ranking p 0.12–0.95.
  - **First half chooses, second half tests (5-minute 49 / 50; 10-minute 33 / 34).** 5-minute: the 100 best first-half rules by t25 mean beat the left-out group in the second half 99 times of 100, median edge +26 points (chance median −2, p 0.010); capped +12.8 (p 0.057); 2× edge +4 points (p 0.36). They share one condition family (fees per $10K traded at least about 1.0, in 66 of 100; top 10 under 22–23% in 29) and one coin: PHISH (+160%) is in 99 of the 100 kept sets, MDAQ (+156%) in 48. **Without the second half's three best buys the edge is +2.5 points and 54% positive.** 10-minute: no carry on returns (50% positive, p 0.30); the first-half "fewest severe losses" rules (top 10 under about 24% in 75 of 100) did carry — 20 points fewer severe losses in the second half, p about 0.02 one-sided, one test of ten — but those rules have a WORSE average (kept −12.3% against +3.0%).
  - **Economics of the shortlist (0.2 SOL a buy, 25% stop):** 5-minute top 10 under 22% AND bundlers under 35%: 19 buys, 9 doubled (47% against 25%, Fisher p 0.09), +68.3% mean, median −13%, 9 won, +2.59 SOL against −2.18 SOL for the 80 left out; without its best one / two / three: +31.4% / +10.7% / +1.3%; halves +149% (7 buys) and +21% (12). Fees per $10K traded at least 1.0: 55 buys, 34.5% doubled against 22.7%, +12.8% mean, −7.8% without its best two. Snipers 7%+: 47 buys, 40% doubled against 19% (Fisher p 0.027, uncorrected), −3.5% without its best two. 10-minute cap $20K+: 22.5% doubled against 7.4%, never rose 10%: 17.5% against 40.7%, +1.2% mean, −16% without its best two.
  - **Fees:** fees and volume are nearly the same measurement (rank correlation 0.78 at 5 minutes, 0.86 at 10). Given volume, fees say nothing about the result (partial rank correlation −0.03 and −0.12). The ratio fees per $10K traded: top third 45.5% doubled against 18% and 24% in the 5-minute book, not repeated at 10 minutes (22% / 14% / 14%).
  - **Exits, paired on the same buys:** 10-minute t20 beats t25 (+4.46 points, 95% +1.2 to +7.7). 5-minute: t20 better on 41 buys, worse on 12, but mean −1.7 points (the big winners). **The 40% stop was worse than the 25% stop on 62 of 99 five-minute buys and better on 17; means −1.0% against +2.1%. The "25% stop too tight" cases reported today (ZBRA, ZCOIN, VINU) are real but the average does not favour the wider stop.** Half-at-2× no better anywhere.
  - **Repeat buys, 10-minute:** after a losing 5-minute buy 26 buys, 3 won, −23.5% mean, median −23% (rest: 41 buys, +7.9%, median −15%; Mann-Whitney p 0.024, uncorrected). **In 25 of the 26 the 5-minute 25% stop had ALREADY sold at a loss before the 10-minute buy was made — the status was known at the decision, no hindsight.** After a winning 5-minute buy: 18 buys, +22.7% mean but −11.5% without Pnut.
  - **Pricing defects (ChatGPT's review of 8fedfb4): polled.mjs and jupiter.mjs have NOT changed since that commit (running 20653a2, review head e221685) — none of the six is fixed. Read in the code and confirmed present:** (1) the urgent and routine passes can both ask about one position and held.set() takes whichever answer arrives last, with no check of request time; (2) a pass waits for all its workers, so one slow request (10 s timeout) delays the next look at every position; (3) the scheduler takes a request off the queue and then waits for credits, so an urgent quote arriving meanwhile waits behind it (about 1 s at most on this plan); (4) the returned input amount is read but never compared with the amount asked; (5) the young positions' share is capped at 10-second looks, so past about 48 young sizes the budget would be exceeded (today 195–215 requests a minute against 384); (6) "n += await" in parallel workers loses counts (a diagnostic only).
  - **Measured effect on today's sales (gaps.py, 91 sales on the 25% stop): the gap between the look that triggered and the look before it was 3.1 s median, 5.1 s at most; 24 of the 26 sales noticed more than 10% past the stop had an ordinary 3-second gap** (FIREBALL: value 0.845 of cost at one look, 0.437 three seconds later). So the late sales are price falls between two normal looks, not defect (2). This also checks, sale by sale, what was said unverified at 19:21. Defects (1), (3), (4) cannot be seen in the published data either way.
  - **Proposed as paper counts only (no code, no rule change, nothing built, waits for his word):** (a) 10-minute: flag the buy when the 5-minute 25% stop on that coin has already sold at a loss — 39% of buys; (b) 10-minute: t20 against t25, already recorded; (c) 5-minute: flag top 10 under 22% AND bundlers under 35% — about one buy in five; judged with and without its best coin. Watch list: fees per $10K traded 1.0+, snipers 7%+ at 5 minutes, cap $20K+ at 10 minutes, top 10 under 24% at 10 minutes as a loss-reducer.

- **26 Nov 22:44 UK — his experiment: wait after MIGRATION, then buy on strength — four variants: 5 minutes after migration if the cap is over $50K; 10 minutes after if over $50K; 5 minutes after if over $100K; 10 minutes after if over $100K ("that kind of avoids the immediate rug on migration").** Not tested by anything we run (the new-coin checks are timed from CREATION, the pool books buy on their own triggers). Testable on stored data first: the server database has the minute bars from migration for every pool coin, so one read-only study can replay all four variants over every coin migrated since 25 Nov (how many buys a day, how many coins are already dead at 5 and 10 minutes, results per stop — from minute bars, so approximate). Today's three rugs as a first sanity check, from their checks (exact migration minutes not in the published files): Trust $50K at 5 min from creation, $108K at 10, $1.5K at 15; MIM $39K / $70K / $104K at 5 / 10 / 15, $1.4K at 20; CONNIE $81K at 5, $102K at 8, $1.5K at 10 — each collapsed roughly 5–10 minutes after it got big, so a 5-minute wait would probably still have bought Trust and MIM near their tops; a 10-minute wait would probably have missed all three. Related evidence: pool-book buys of fresh migrated coins at $100–300K: 34 buys, −9.0%, 29% won; at $300K+: 25 buys, −5.8%, 40% reached 2×. Proposed: the study first, then paper books for the variants that survive it. Waits for his go.
- **26 Nov 22:46 UK — his go: "If you have the stored data, let's replay it" — 5 and 10 minutes after migration, buy if over $50K; the over-$100K buys are part of those (reported as their own band, no separate rule).** Built and pushed to the review branch as **d7210db** (older-11-review): `older/scripts/study-migration.mjs` (read-only; opens the run's database, replays the rule over every migration the run saw from the one-minute bars, writes pub/study-migration.json for the publisher, prints a summary) + `older/src/migstudy.mjs` (the arithmetic) + tests (283 pass). Nothing in the running bot changes; no provider is asked. How a sale is priced from minute bars: "best case" = at the stop level; "cautious" = at the close of the minute the stop was hit in when that minute closed under the level; no fee or price impact. Migration moment: the pool-creation second where the coin first became known that way, otherwise the minute of the pool's first trade for coins the new-coin books already knew; coins that had migrated before the run began are left out. Cap = bar price × 1,000,000,000, checked in the output against the bot's own buys. Checked end to end on a made-up database (a runner, a rug, a coin dead at 5 minutes, two coins correctly left out). His paste: cd /root/older-review / git fetch -q origin older-11-review && git checkout -q FETCH_HEAD / node older/scripts/study-migration.mjs valid11 — then the file is published within 10 minutes and read from the data branch.
- **26 Nov 23:01 UK — THE MIGRATION REPLAY, run by him (study-migration.mjs at d7210db, database valid11, 25 Nov 13:14 – 26 Nov 23:00; file study-migration.json on the data branch; scratchpad/mg1–mg4.py).** 839 migrations the run saw in 1.41 days. Supply check passed (cap ÷ price on 293 of the bot's own buys: median 971 million).
  - **What a migration looks like in this market.** Cap in the coin's first pool minute: 263 coins (31%) at the normal migration price, $20–50K (nearly all $28–33K); 481 (57%) already under $20K — 403 under $1K (dumped in the same minute as the pool appeared, or old dead coins: the 113 "known before" rows are mostly these and are not buys of any rule); 95 (11%) already over $50K in the first minute, 19 of them showing $1M+ and never moving. **187 of the 263 normal migrations happened within 2 minutes of the coin's creation** (46 in the same minute, 141 at 1–2 minutes): the curve is bought out at launch. So "N minutes after migration" is nearly "N minutes after creation" — the new-coin books' own clock.
  - **Where the 263 normal migrations are afterwards (dead = under $5K):** 5 min: 12% dead, 11% over $50K · 10 min: 42% dead, 18% over $50K · 15 min: 56% dead · 30 min: 72% dead, 19% over $50K · 60 min: 84% dead, 10% over $50K, 3.4% over $100K. A coin over $100K at 10 minutes: 56% dead within the hour. Still $50K+ at 30 minutes: 41–58% still $50K+ at 60.
  - **The two rules (buy if over $50K), 25% stop:** 5 minutes after: 114 buys (81 a day), cautious −6.3% a trade (median −9.7%, 46 won), best case +6.7%; 31.6% reached 2×; 75.4% went on to fall 90%. 10 minutes after: 123 buys (87 a day), cautious −12.3% (44 won), best case +5.5%; 26.8% reached 2×; 77.2% fell 90%. First half of the period against the second (5-minute rule): +3.2% then −15.8% cautious. 40% stop: −10.6% and −19.2% cautious. The band that looked good in the printout — 10 minutes after, $100–300K, 27 buys, +10.2% — is −10.8% without its best two buys (one coin went 10×). The 19 "$1M+ in the first minute" coins: 74% never rose 10%, −19.1%.
  - **Readings (fees / top 10 / bundlers / phishing):** only 12 and 21 of the buys had one — nothing can be said.
  - **TODAY'S $100K RUNNERS: 18 of the list's coins are in the replay; 12 were under $5K an hour after migrating, 6 were still $50K or more** (CI ×2, GTA 6 Coin, SpaceXCoin, Kuro, HOLD). Dead within the hour: WIENER, Anthropic, BASKET, Zero, X, Nike, Life (a "runner bought"), Manager, Trust, AMUSK, CONNIE, SpaceX. **Two thirds of the "missed runners" were pump-and-pull coins — the missed-runner tally has been counting mostly these.**

(2) 22:04 — Corleone "a real miss, held at $240K": it was $269K at 10 minutes, $202K at 15 and $1.9K at 30 — also a pull. (3) MIM yPe7gB is not in the replay (its migration was not classified); not checked further.
  - **Reading: the replay does not support either rule. These coins do not rug at migration — they are pumped first and pulled 5 to 30 minutes later, so "over $50K a few minutes after migration" selects exactly the coins being pumped.** Not yet tested: waiting until the pull window has passed (15 / 30 / 60 minutes after migration and still over $50K) — the same script with more waiting times; proposed, waits for his go.

- **27 Nov 07:17 UK — 30-minute check (state 07:06:22 UTC, publish 07:09:50; code 20653a2, up 13 h 50 min; not restarted). NOT fine: the status-dump stall now trips the bot's own lateness guard, and the GMGN background readings have all failed since 06:36.**
  - **Measured.** Stall 4.06–4.28 s every five minutes, with 4.55 s at 06:35 and **5.09 s at 06:40**. The stream's lateness limit is 5 s (FEED_MAX_LAG_S); its ordinary lag is about 1 s, so a 4-second freeze puts it at the limit. **Late spells: 28 in the run** (the published list holds the last ten: nine between 06:36:37 and 06:41:34, one at 07:01:30; each about 5 s, one 10.3 s). 12,850 feed events skipped as late; 143 s spent "recovering" (no pool-book signal or pricing in that time).
  - **GMGN background ("for the record") readings: 188 asked since 06:36, 188 failed** ("did not arrive within 60 s"); failed 160 → 348, answered stuck at 6,974. This is the second gap (first 06:22–06:29) and it has not ended. **It is a queue, not a ban:** the wait for a background reading was about 5 s all night (median 4.6–6.9 s from 23:00 to 04:30), then 7.6 s (04:30), 9.9 s (05:00), 19.4 s (05:30), 25 s (06:00), 49 s (06:30), then over the 60-second give-up limit. GMGN calls an hour rose from 650–1,000 before 04:26 to 1,170–1,430 since — the most the 2-second pace allows — while the readings asked for did not rise (115–190 per half hour). "Too many requests" answers 11 → 15; banned 0.
  - **Buy readings are in their own lane and untouched:** 8 more asked, 8 answered (382 / 379 / 3 refused; about 0.1 s each). No buy decision affected. What is lost is the fees / top 10 / bundlers / phishing label on coins refused for other reasons — the "why missed" record for runners.
  - **Why (from the code, not yet confirmed on the server):** every feed drop or late spell opens a coverage gap, and main.mjs then starts a gap-repair pass (`backfill.patchGap`) that asks GMGN for one-minute bars of every coin with a position or a cap between $20K and $1M, in the same background lane as the readings. The passes are not queued behind each other; several run at once. Gap repair so far: 927 patched, 7,300 asked and not usable. Feed drop no. 4 (about 04:10–04:26) started one pass (wait 5 → 8–10 s); the late spells since then added more.
  - **What fixes it: the restart on d7210db** (in the server's folder since 26 Nov): the dump no longer freezes the bot, so no late spells from it, and the queue starts empty. Told him first, with the two lines. A code weakness remains for later, on his go: gap-repair passes should run one at a time and behind the readings.

  - **Baseline, buying at the look:** the 20% trailing stop is the least bad exit in every book and overall (all 300: 20% −3.0%, −1.82 SOL; 25% −4.5%; 30% −11.5%; 40% −11.2%; 60% −37.0%; 70% −54.3%; half-at-2× rows no better; buy-price stops −32% to −47%). No recorded exit is positive in any book.
  - **Exits alone do not do it:** 280 exit variants per book simulated exactly from the rows (take-profit at 1.1× … 7× on 25 / 50 / 75 / 100% of the position over each trailing stop, and three-step ladders): none positive on all buys in 5M, 10M, 20M or pooled; one in 15M (+1.2% on 26 buys, old +21% / new −44%).
  - **Entry conditions alone do not do it:** 4,599 one-condition rules (coin figures, hour, market mood from the last 5 / 10 / 20 sold buys, buys in the last 30 / 60 minutes, earlier buy of the same coin) × three exits: an edge on the old buys does not predict an edge on the new ones (correlation 0.01); 133 rules positive on both periods where chance alone gives about 176; 3 stay positive without their best two coins.
  - **ONE CONDITION CARRIED: fee density (10,000 × fees SOL / traded $).** Threshold 1.0 was fixed in round 1, so the 90 new buys are an honest test: fee ≥ 1.0 new 48 buys −2.2% on the 20% stop against −12.8% for fee < 1.0 (42 buys), difference +10.5 points p 0.04; on the 25% stop +16.4 points p 0.013. Dose: under 0.70 −10.8% (16 buys, 20% stop); 0.70–0.85 −14.7% (54; 9% reached 2×); 0.85–1.00 −0.1% (69); 1.00–1.15 +2.3% (79); 1.15–1.40 −1.7% (57; 36% reached 2×); 1.40+ −0.6% (25; 36%). A loss-reducer to about break-even (fee ≥ 1.0 all 161 buys: +0.4% on the 20% stop, +1.3% on the 25%), not a profit by itself. Works in 5M (+7.1% against −11.1%) and 20M (+4.2% against −12.9%), not in 10M (−4.8% against +3.2%).

  - **STRATEGY "CONFIRM" (new, frozen today as a paper view):** the books' signal stays as it is, but no buy at the look; buy only when the position value reaches RISE × cost; the order is cancelled if the price falls 20% from its high first; exit = 20% trailing stop. Exact from the rows: it trades iff the 20% row's peak ≥ RISE, and from that moment the stop sells at the recorded moment and price; return = (1 + ret.t20) / (RISE × (1 + entry cost)) − 1. The entry cost over the trigger (round-trip fees about 3% + the overshoot of a 3-second look) is NOT measured: 6% assumed, 3% and 10% shown.

Hit = first fresh crossing up through the line within 8 hours of migration (age group A0). NOW = bought at that observation, priced from the pool state like every pool book (fee and price impact included, no Jupiter request). 5M / 10M = judged 5 / 10 minutes after the hit: bought only if the cap is at or over the line then, otherwise recorded "fell under, no buy". One buy per coin per book, 0.2 SOL, every stop row, 24 hours, gates as labels. Detail on each buy: line_usd, wait_s, hit_t, hit_mc, prev_mc, since_mig_s, vol_since_mig_usd, late_s, mc_at_wait.
  - engine.mjs: `migrated(mint, t)` (called by the feed at the pool-creation event) starts the clock and, with a HIT book on, asks for the coin's transfer-fee reading at once instead of at its first decision (reader latency measured today: mean 0.52 s, 201 of 208 within 1 s); the HIT pass runs in onEvent BEFORE the creation-time check (these books need no creation time) and only when there is something to buy, judge or record; a hit while the fee is unread is not bought and is counted (`hitFeeUnread`) and noted. Books off = nothing evaluated, behaviour as before (the 289 earlier tests unchanged apart from the row counts 138 → 144 book-ages and 35 → 41 families).
  - main.mjs: the published buy record gains `hit` (the detail above). page.mjs: the six rule texts.
  - Not restored after a restart: only pools created after a start are followed; a wait still running at a restart is dropped (one-buy marks are kept as for every book).
  - Cost: priced from the live pool feed (no Jupiter quote polling); per buy one GMGN buy reading and the usual background snapshot; about 350 buys a day expected from the replay. The fee reading: the same single read per coin, made a few seconds earlier — to be said to him because of "do not increase Helius".

- **27 Nov 16:10 UK — the site is hard to scroll on his iPad (his report 15:59: "try to move it, it doesn't move"). Cause found and measured; fix built LOCALLY (branch page-light, 40cbd39, 306 tests), NOT pushed — waiting for his go.**
  - **Cause:** the page rebuilds its whole list of books — 33 cards, about 26,000 elements, 1 MB of HTML — at every price update of an open position, i.e. every 2 seconds, and again at the 10-second refresh. Measured on a copy of the site fed with the run's own 895 buys (scratchpad sim/serve.mjs + measure.mjs + idle.mjs; iPad viewport, Chromium with the processor slowed 4×): frozen 88% of the time while scrolling (longest freeze 2.1 s; 19 of ~600 scroll steps got through) and 83% left alone (longest 2.4 s). At 2× slowed 51%; unslowed 21%. A simulated tablet, not his own iPad.
  - **Today's build made an existing problem worse, it did not create it:** without the 4 follow-up books and the 10 views (19 book cards instead of 33) the same test gives 71% frozen (4×) / 41% (2×).
  - Also found: the page downloaded the whole state (1.5 MB) every 10 seconds only for the health strip — about 11 MB a minute, plus 6.7 MB a minute for the books — and each such request made the bot build every result row again.
  - **Fix (page-light):** a card is three parts and a part is replaced only when its content changed; no redraw while the page is touched or scrolled (or for a second after); UK times use three shared formatters and are written once (a new formatter per time per redraw was the largest part of the page's own work: redraw 158 → 73 ms); the health strip asks a new light /api/health (status + disk, about 150 KB); /api/books is sent compressed (944 → 206 KB). **Same test after: frozen 7% while scrolling (longest 0.24 s, 502 of ~600 steps), 19% left alone (longest 0.43 s).** Layout identical (screenshots compared). Needs a restart of the bot to take effect.

Think carefully and deeply"). Method: scratchpad vol/lib.py, a1–a5.py on the published entries (state 17:53 UK); 93 filled buys of 88 coins, 25 Nov 16:09 – 27 Nov 17:31 UTC (run valid11); a buy shared by two or three of the books counted once. Earlier record read first: 22–24 Nov (older-8/10 data) VOL300K 14 buys 7% won −1.81 SOL, VOL500K 8 buys 0 won −1.32 SOL, "the three $1.9–2.0M buys with $567K–$928K traded lost 69%, 76%, 99%… right at the top of the $2M band".**
  - **Parameters given to him first (17:50):** trigger = $100K / $300K / $500K traded in the last 5 minutes (window fully observed); migrated pump.fun coins, any age to 90 days; cap $40K–$2M; price direction not checked; 0.2 SOL one second after the signal at the pool price; once per coin per age group; re-buys off; every stop recorded. The GMGN reading (top 10, bundlers, insiders, snipers, fees, Twitter, ATH; holders and phishing unlabelled; dev holding stored, not shown) is a LABEL only. **All 91 buys with a reading FAIL the label checks as written** (insiders 2.5%+ fails 89, top 10 18–30% fails 77, fees 5 SOL fails 60): those checks cannot serve as a filter here.
  - **Overall:** 20% stop −19.1% a buy, 19 winners (20%), −3.56 SOL; 25% −20.1%; 40% −31.8%; 60% −56.1%. 17 buys lost more than half even on the 20% stop.
  - **ONE CLEAR SPLIT — "dead coins with made-up volume": 20 of the 93 buys, 0 winners, −72.4% a buy on the 20% stop (−2.90 SOL = 81% of the whole loss); the other 73: −4.5% a buy (−0.66 SOL), 19 winners.** Mann-Whitney p 1e-10; winners 0 of 20 against 19 of 73, Fisher p 0.010. What they are: old coins (15 of 20 over 36 hours; the other 73 are 70 under 8 hours) whose cap is under 15% of the provider's all-time high (median 1.2%; ATH $4M–$821M) and, in 13 of 20, sits at $1.88–2.00M — just under the books' $2M ceiling; the 5-minute volume of $100K–$715K comes from a handful of wallets (median 12 buyers; $4K–$65K each — the other 73: median 876 buyers, about $165 each); about 2,000–3,600 holders with the top 10 holding 0.3–0.6%; 60–100% phishing wallets where read; fees under 2.5 SOL; no Twitter link on 17 of 20; the names repeat (UBR ×3, GgJedw ×2, UBI). 15 of 20 never showed a value above 0.99× of cost: the paper buy is under water at once and sold at −47% to −100% on every stop. The same pattern as the three $1.9–2.0M buys of 22–24 Nov: it holds across two runs and on both days it occurred in this one (26 Nov 16 buys −74%, 27 Nov 4 buys −67%; none on 25 Nov).
  - **Rules that take that group out (each alone; KEEP the buy if…):** cap at least 15% of its ATH (GMGN) → keeps 73 at −4.5%, removes exactly the 20, no winner lost; **at least 200 different buyers in the 5 minutes (the bot's own feed: free, instant, never late) → keeps 73 at −4.9%, removes 18 of the 20 plus two Fireball buys (−1%, −6%), no winner lost**; under 8 hours old → keeps 71 at −5.1%; Twitter link → keeps 73 at −6.3% (17 of 20); top 10 at least 3% → keeps 70 at −4.9% but loses 2 winners; cap under $1.8M → only 13 of 20; phishing under 50% → 14 of 20 (not read on the early buys). Both of the first two together: keeps 71 at −4.5% (−0.64 SOL), 19 winners.
  - **The other 73 — winners (19, +22.7% average) against losers (54, −14.1%; 31 between 0 and −15%, 22 between −15 and −30%, one −98%: wif): NO figure separates them.** 38 figures compared (cap, cap ÷ ATH, 5-minute and 1-hour volume, volume ÷ cap and ÷ liquidity, liquidity, buyers in 1 and 5 minutes, volume per buyer, largest buyer's share, traders, transactions, buys ÷ sells, net buying, holders, holder change, top 10, bundlers, insiders, snipers, fees, fees per $10K, phishing, bot and fresh wallets, hour): the best is the hour of day (AUC 0.65, p 0.06); about two would reach p 0.05 by chance and none does. Hints only, on small groups that overlap: cap $300–700K 20 buys +5.9%; fees under 1 SOL 26 buys +1.5%; "operator" coins (bundlers and snipers both 40%+, fees ~0.2 SOL, cap $430–870K: the …ball coins, Grodia, NBA) 16 buys +5.8% with 8 of 16 later at 2×; cap under $100K 23 buys −12.0%.
  - **The sells.** Tighter is better at every step: trailing 20% −4.5%, 25% −5.5%, 30% −12.2%, 40% −19.8%, 60% −49.7% (the 73); half-at-2× slightly better (20% −3.7%); a stop from the buy price far worse (−23% to −83%). **23 of the 73 (32%) were later worth 2× the buy within 24 hours (8 of them 3×+: DTSkmo 8.4×, Hoppy 7.2×, Fireball 7.1×, KAI 6.5×, MONEY 4.8×…), and the 20% stop had already sold 15 of those 23 at a loss.** So the runners are in these buys, but they dip 20%+ first — and a wider stop loses more on the rest than it gains on them. The published files do not hold the path (how deep, how long before the run); the bars are in the database on the server.
  - **By book (a shared buy in each):** VOL100K 88 buys −19.3% (−3.40 SOL); without the dead group 69 buys −4.3%, 19 winners. VOL300K 12 buys, 7 dead, −53.0%; the other 5: −9.2%, no winner. VOL500K 5 buys, 4 dead, −64.5%; the other one −8.0%. No buy at the $300K or $500K level has won in this run (0 of 14); the earlier run was the same (VOL500K 0 of 8, VOL300K 1 of 14).
  - **Proposed to him (waiting for his go; nothing changed):** (1) the three books buy only when at least 200 different wallets bought in the 5 minutes AND the cap is at least 15% of the coin's all-time high — hindsight on 93 buys, but a mechanism (made-up volume in a dead coin), an effect of 68 points, no winner lost, and the same thing seen in the earlier run; (2) a read-only replay on the server of what the price did after each volume signal (how deep the dip, how long to the high) before touching the exits — the "sold, then ran" question cannot be answered from the published files.

- **27 Nov 18:08 UK — VOLUME BOOKS, SECOND PASS: "get rid of the obvious rugs and tell me a strategy which will be profitable" (his request 18:02 UK; discussion only, nothing built).** Method: scratchpad vol2/b1–b4.py on the published entries (state 18:03 UK, commit b0f0b0d); the clean set = buys passing BOTH rug rules (200+ buyers in the 5 minutes AND cap 15%+ of ATH): **71 buys of 67 coins, 25 Nov 16:09 – 27 Nov 17:31 UTC** (94 filled, 21 in the dead group).
  - **Answer given: no rule in this data can be called profitable.** Clean set on the 20% stop: −4.5% a buy, −0.64 SOL, 19 winners, 90% range of the mean −9% to 0%. The whole positive side is four buys (Grodia +119%, MONEY +62%, Cateball +42%, Santaball +42% = +2.65 stakes); the other 67 average −8.8%.
  - **Exits (40 tested).** Recorded rows: t20 −4.5%, h20 −3.7%, t25 −6.3%, t30 −12.9%, t40 −18.7%. Fixed target + trailing stop (target reached before the stop sold, worked out from the stop's own result: peak ≥ (1 + result) ÷ (1 − stop); a floor): best is sell at +10% with the 20% stop = −2.6% (43 of 71 reached it; 49 needed to break even); +20% target with the 25% stop −2.9%; every wider stop worse (−8% to −15%). **No exit turns the clean set positive.** Highest value within 24 h (after any sale too): 61 of 71 reached 1.1×, 51 1.2×, 45 1.3×, 30 1.5×, 21 2×, 7 3× — but before the 20% stop sold only 6 had passed 1.5× and 28 had not passed 1.1×: most of the rise comes after the stop has already sold, and a wider stop does not catch it (t40 −18.7%).
  - **Entry filters, searched exhaustively with a luck test.** 330 single cuts (37 figures × 5 levels × both sides, 15+ buys) and 29,591 pairs. Best single on the 20% stop: under 1,036 sells in the last hour, 18 buys +9.4% — shuffled results give a "best" that good in 15% of tries (typical best by luck +6.8%); best pair +13.9% on 15 buys, by luck in 35% of tries. Same on t25, h20 and both target exits (14–94%). Only the 40% stop shows something beyond luck (holders up 85%+ in the 5 minutes, 18 buys +12.6%, 2%) — one of six exits tried, so not safe. **Forward test:** the ten best single rules chosen on the first half (average +12.4% there) made +1.6% on the second half (second half as a whole −3.0%); pairs +17.2% → +1.2%. So a filter picked from this data keeps about a tenth of its apparent edge.
  - **What does hold: the small-cap buys are the losers.** Cap under $300K at the buy: 42 buys −9.4% (−0.79 SOL), 90% range −15% to −5%, both halves negative (−12% / −7%), negative on every exit. Cap $300K+: 29 buys +2.5% (+0.14 SOL), 90% range −6% to +12%, halves +2% / +3%, **−1.7% without its best buy**. Cap under $100K: 23 buys −12.0%. Likely mechanism: $100K traded in 5 minutes on a coin worth under $100–300K is the whole coin changing hands = the top of a pump. Related cuts say the same thing (fewer than 4,100 trades in the hour 18 buys +8.2%; holders up 70%+ in the 5 minutes 24 buys +4.9%, 0.0% without its best; 5-minute volume $100–120K 35 buys −9.7%).
  - **The $300K+ profit is the operator coins.** Bundlers and snipers both 40%+ (…ball coins, Grodia, NBA; fees 0.1–0.3 SOL, top 10 40–50%, cap $470–850K): 16 buys +5.8%, −1.7% without Grodia; $300K+ and NOT operator: 14 buys −1.7%. LESSONS (21–22 Oct): live stop sales on bundle-launch dumps landed 6–58% below paper — so this group's paper result is the least trustworthy part.
  - **By day / hour / level:** 25 Nov −4.5% (24), 26 Nov −4.9% (33), 27 Nov −3.7% (14): no day carries it. 20:00–24:00 UK 23 buys +2.8%; 16:00–20:00 25 buys −9.1%. $300K / $500K levels after the rug rules: 6 buys, none won.
  - **Sample size:** the clean set's spread is 25 points a buy (standard error 2.9 on 71). To tell the $300K+ group's +2.5% from zero needs about 380 buys = about four weeks at the present rate.
  - **Suggested to him (nothing built; waiting):** (1) the two rug rules, as before — the only solid finding (81% of the loss, 0 winners lost, seen in two runs); (2) cap $300K+ as a CARD on the page (a view, buying unchanged) so it is tested forward instead of trusted from hindsight; (3) keep the 20% stop, do not widen; (4) the read-only replay of the price path after each signal — 21 of 71 doubled within 24 hours, mostly after the stop had sold, so if a profitable rule exists here it is in WHEN to buy (after the first dip / on a new high), which the published files cannot test; (5) VOL300K / VOL500K add nothing over VOL100K so far.

- **27 Nov 19:33 UK — VOLUME BOOKS, THIRD PASS: ChatGPT's "research handover" checked and its multivariable programme run (he pasted the handover 19:28 UK). Method: scratchpad vol3/common.py, v1–v4.py on the published entries (publish 19:24 UTC, bot b0f0b0d); the 95 buys of the review CSV, clean set 72; no new volume buy since the snapshot (last id 1050), so no out-of-sample test was possible.**
  - **ChatGPT's figures all reproduce** (it did read the file): 95 buys / 90 mints, t20 −19.52% (−3.708 SOL at 0.2); buyers ≥200 → 74 at −5.04%; cap/ATH ≥15% → 74 at −4.60%; both → 72 at −4.63%; the 24 exits; operator coins 17 at +4.77% (−2.35% without the best, halves +5.74 / +4.09); cap under $300K 42 at −9.38%, $300K+ 30 at +2.02% (operator 16 at +5.31%, others 14 at −1.75%); its three best single rules (+9.38 / +8.75%). Two caveats on its tables: phishing / bot / fresh-wallet shares are missing on 33 of the 72 clean buys (not read on the early buys), so those cuts rest on 39 buys; and "$ per buyer under $86: 14 buys, no winner" is within luck — a 20%-cut that poor turns up in 88% of shuffles (80 cuts).
  - **Break-even arithmetic of the clean set (20% stop):** 19 winners of 72 (26.4%) averaging +22.7%; 53 losers averaging −14.4% (30 between 0 and −15%, 22 between −15 and −30%, 1 worse). To break even it needs 38.8% winners at these sizes, or winners of +40% at this rate. After the rug rules there are almost no catastrophic losses left to filter: the loss is many small stop-outs.
  - **Exhaustive search with the whole search repeated on shuffled results (2,000 shuffles; rows shuffled whole so a buy's exits stay together).** 40 figures in three bands each (low / middle / high third, so middle ranges are tested), 5 exits (t20, t25, t30, t40, h20): 119 single bands, 1,389 pairs (12+ buys), 7,184 triples (10+ buys) = 43,460 rule-and-exit combinations. Best single +6.5% (shuffles match or beat it 61%); best pair +27.0% (5.3%); **best triple +40.8% (0.6%); with every buy capped at +50% so no single trade can carry a rule: single 64%, pair 13%, triple 0.1%.** First search in this project to beat its own luck test.
  - **What the winning cell is: "holders up more than 70% in the 5 minutes AND more than $72 traded per trade over the last hour" (1-hour volume ÷ 1-hour trades), on the 40% stop: 12 buys of 12 different coins, 8 won, +27.0% (+0.65 SOL); without the best +18.8%, without the best three +1.5%; first half +51.0% (4), second half +15.0% (8); by day 25 Nov +53% (3), 26 Nov +34% (4), 27 Nov +6% (5).** On the 20% stop the same 12: +17.1%, 5 won, −1.9% without the best three. Thresholds are not knife-edge: holders +30% to +120% and $40 to $120 a trade give +12% to +43% on the 40% stop wherever the cell keeps 10–17 buys. The rest of the clean set on the 40% stop: 60 buys −28.1%; launch burst with small trades 12 buys −17.3%; big trades without the burst 12 buys −28.7%.
  - **The cell is two serial launchers, not twelve independent coins.** (a) "BARE" template — bundlers and snipers about 0%, top 10 25–38%, fees 0.7–2.5 SOL, 570–1,700 holders growing 200–570% in five minutes, cap $450K–1.2M, $145–440 a trade: MONEY (two mints), KURO, KAI, 94Dwo5, 8sAxws = 6 in the cell: 40% stop +53.8%, 5 of 6 won; 20% stop +6.7%, 2 won (they dip 20–40% first: KURO −7% on the 20% stop, +117% on the 30%+; KAI −2% / +50%). All 8 BARE buys in the clean set: 40% stop +35.6% (5 won; halves +24.0 / +47.1), 20% stop +2.0%; one was pulled to zero later (94Dwo5: −100% on the 60% stop). (b) Operator coins (bundlers = snipers 58–73%: Grodia, Cateball, Santaball, Dogeball, Fireball) = 5 in the cell: 20% stop +35.8%, 40% stop +7.4% — they need the TIGHT stop. The 47 clean buys that are neither type: −9.2% on the 20% stop, −28.9% on the 40%, negative in both halves on every exit. So the shuffle test overstates the evidence: it treats 12 buys as independent, and the launcher decides when the run ends.
  - **Fees:** no relation to the result — rank correlation of total fees and of fees ÷ cap, ÷ liquidity, ÷ holders, ÷ volume with the 20% and 40% results between −0.05 and +0.03 (p 0.66–0.95); after taking out cap, liquidity and trade count, +0.07 to +0.24 (two of twelve at p 0.04–0.05, uncorrected). By band: under 0.5 SOL 20 buys +2.9% (the operator coins), every band from 0.5 SOL up −5% to −11%. Coin age is not in the published file (only the age group, 69 of 72 under 8 hours), so fees per hour of age cannot be tested.
  - **Volume level:** higher is not better. 5-minute volume $100–120K (just over the trigger) 36 clean buys −9.8%; $120–160K 18 buys +5.2%; $160–300K 12 buys −1.7%; $300K+ 6 buys −9.0%, no winner (all buys at $300K+: 15, −50.4%, 9 of them dead-group). Volume ÷ cap under 0.25: 20 buys +2.8%; over 1: 31 buys −9.4%. One-hour net buying $20K+: 28 buys +2.8%; under $20K: 44 buys −9.4%.
  - **Rank correlations, 40 figures:** with the 20% result none reaches p 0.05 (2 expected by luck); with the 40% result four do — holder growth +0.37 (p 0.001; survives a 40-fold correction), $ per trade +0.29, 5-minute volume +0.27, net buying +0.24.
  - **Models scored only on buys they had not seen:** for the 20% stop nothing (leave-one-out AUC 0.32 logistic, 0.47 boosted trees; top third picked −10.0% / −2.0%). For the 40% stop some separation (AUC 0.60–0.66; first half → second half 0.68–0.75): the bottom third −25%, but the top third picked only −2.7% to +4.1% — they find the buys a wide stop destroys, not a profitable set.
  - **Verdict given to him: NO VALIDATED PROFITABLE STRATEGY. One exploratory candidate, the first to pass a luck test.** Frozen at 19:33 UK 27 Nov so that every volume buy after id 1050 is a true forward test — F1 "FRESH-BIG": volume signal AND cap ÷ ATH ≥ 0.15 AND buyers_5m ≥ 200 AND Jupiter holders change over 5 minutes > 70% AND 1-hour volume ÷ 1-hour trades > $72; judged on the 40% stop (20% reported beside it). F2 "BARE" (subset, defined after looking, weaker): clean AND bundlers < 3% AND snipers < 3% AND fees < 3 SOL AND top 10 ≥ 24%; 40% stop. F3 "OPER": clean AND bundlers ≥ 40% AND snipers ≥ 40%; 20% stop. To be reported at every check from the published file (no code needed); a card on the page needs his go. LESSONS still applies: live stop sales on one-launcher coins landed 6–58% below paper in October, and a wide stop is where that bites.

- **27 Nov 19:40 UK — his request (19:39 UK): "we're trying to find a refined new book for these coins… what practical steps to take forward". PROPOSAL GIVEN (nothing built; waiting for his go).** The idea in one line: stop treating every volume signal alike — after the rug rules only two coin types make money and they need opposite exits; the other 47 of 72 clean buys lose on every exit. Proposed plan: (1) one push, no change to what is bought except the rug rule — (a) hard rule in the three volume books: at least 200 different buyers in the 5 minutes (own feed, instant; removed 18 of the 20 dead buys, no winner); cap ÷ ATH ≥ 15% stays a label until the reading is shown to arrive in time; (b) three cards on the page from figures the bot already records (no extra provider calls): VOL-CLEAN (passes both rug rules; 72 buys −4.6% on the 20% stop), VOL-FRESH = frozen rule F1 (holders up 70%+ in 5 minutes AND over $72 a trade in the last hour; 12 buys, 40% stop +27.0%, 20% stop +17.1%), VOL-REST (clean but not FRESH: 60 buys, −9.0% on the 20% stop, −28.1% on the 40%); (c) the status-build fix in the same paste. (2) Forward test with the pass line fixed in advance: after 30 new VOL-FRESH buys (about 5–6 days at 6 a day) it passes only if the 40% stop is above +5% a buy AND still positive without its best two AND at least 40% of buys win; expected honestly at a few percent, not +27% (rules picked from this data have kept about a tenth of their edge). (3) The price-path export, to set the exit per type (the template coins dip 20–40% then run; the operator coins need the tight stop) and to test small targets / buying after the dip. (4) Only if it passes: make VOL-FRESH a real book with its own entry rules and stop, and retire VOL300K / VOL500K and the plain signal. Scored run only on his word.

At about 5.7 control buys a day, 200 buys is about five weeks. A full trade-by-trade price record for 24 hours per buy is a bigger piece of work than an export from the one-minute bars the bot already keeps: proposed to start with the export. **Build waits for his go.**

CONTROL = clean AND Jupiter holder change over 5 minutes > 70% AND 1-hour volume ÷ 1-hour trades > $72 — its own exit t40. BARE = CONTROL with a reading IN TIME showing bundlers < 3%, snipers < 3%, fees < 3 SOL, top 10 ≥ 24% — h40. OPER = CONTROL with bundlers ≥ 40% and snipers ≥ 40% in time — t20, t25 beside it. REST = clean, not CONTROL — t20. ADAPTIVE (a line on CONTROL): BARE on h40, every other CONTROL buy on t20. A missing or late reading → type UNKNOWN (kept apart from NEITHER). A buy whose buyers, all-time high, holder growth or 1-hour trade size was not recorded → UNCLASSED, on no card, counted on CONTROL. One row per buy (a buy shared by two or three volume books counts once; a second buy of the same coin by another volume book a minute later is its own buy, as in the research).

### 27 Nov, 21:31–21:40 UK — "all the data disappeared": found and fixed (5ec009e)

- He pasted his page text (21:30:50): every book "—", 0 trades; header read "60% stop · stake 0.5 SOL · **rest of Solana (sampled)**".
- **Cause (page bug, nothing lost):** the "coins" filter (all / pump.fun live / rest of Solana) is hidden for this run, but its buttons were
  drawn for a moment before the run was known. One tap there saved "rest of Solana" in his browser; this run has no such buys → every
  card empty, and no button to go back. Reproduced on a copy of the live data: 0 rows with that filter.
- **Fix 5ec009e** (328 tests, fail 0): the filter starts hidden; where the run does not offer it, a saved choice is dropped and the page
  reads every coin again by itself. Checked with his exact saved selection: the page recovers, his 60% stop and 0.5 stake kept.
- His page also showed the bot still on 340aa78 (old VOL cards listed, no topic buttons). One paste now brings 0f48f4c + 8ce9609 + 5ec009e.
- Lesson: a selector that can be hidden must never keep filtering; a saved selection has to be checked against what the run offers.

### 27 Nov, 22:39 UK — FARM-90K: "just sell at 2–3×" tested on its own 27 closed buys (looking back; NOT built)

- Owner: several buys were 2–3× up and were then lost on the fall; "maybe we should just sell for 2, 3×".
- Method (publish 22:34; 0.2 SOL; scratchpad `farm_tp.py`): a buy "reached k× while held" = exactly known for 2× (the half-at-2× row differs
  from the trailing row; checked, 0 mismatches with the estimate), for other levels from the target / the highest seen for a buy held into
  its collapse / (1+result)÷(1−stop) for a stop sale. Rule tested: **sell everything at k× or at the $90K target, whichever comes first**.

  | sale capped at | 20% stop | won | without top 3 coins | 70% stop | won | without top 3 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | (rule as it is: $90K target) | +1.702 SOL | 8 of 27 | −1.511 | +1.852 | 9 | −1.360 |
  | 2× | +0.734 | 14 | +0.134 | +0.782 | 15 | +0.182 |
  | 2.5× | +1.635 | 13 | +0.735 | +1.785 | 14 | +0.883 |
  | 3× | +1.490 | 11 | +0.290 | +1.641 | 12 | +0.441 |
  | 4× | +0.289 | 8 | −1.511 | +0.440 | 9 | −1.360 |

- **Where it comes from: the cheap buys.** Bought under $25K (target 3.8–8×): 8 buys, ALL 8 reached 2×, 6 reached 3×; on the rule 3 won
  (Gold +620%, CATE +520%, CATE +466%) and 5 lost 87–100% after being 2.8–3.6× up (Snowball 3.20×, Gold 4-wallet 3.29×, SPACEXLIFE 2.77×,
  SPACEXLIFE 3.58×, SpaceX 2.80×); net +2.237 SOL. Bought at $25K+ (target 2.2–2.9×): 19 buys, 6 reached 2×, 5 won, net −0.535 SOL.
- Reading: his point holds at **2.5–3×, not at 2×**. A 2.5× cap makes the same total as the target rule (+1.64 vs +1.70) from 13 winners
  instead of 8 and stays positive without the three best coins (+0.74 vs −1.51). 2× gives up more than half the profit. 4× changes nothing useful.
- Limits: 27 buys in about 7 hours; four levels tried and the best picked looking back; the whole difference is 8 cheap buys; prices are
  sampled every 3 s, and a sale at the level needs a route at that moment (target sales so far fetched their target: +179% on a 2.76× target).
- Proposed, NOT built (waiting for his word): leave FARM-90K as it is (it is the measurement) and add two paper books on the same buy rule,
  sale capped at 2.5× and at 3× (or the $90K target if lower); compare on new buys.

### 28 Nov, 00:10 UK — live liquidity beside every open position (pushed; waiting for his paste)

- **Owner 27 Nov 23:53:** live liquidity next to the open positions, "because the moment they take liquidity out, the coin dumps… so I can sell if I need to".
- Built (display only; 335 tests, fail 0): a **"liquidity now"** column beside the sell buttons — the figure, the figure at the buy and
  the change (amber from −20%, red from −50%), refreshed with the live prices every 2 s.
  - pool-priced positions (VOL / FAST / RUG / HIT): the pool itself (2 × its SOL side) at every trade; free.
  - quote-priced positions (the looks, CONFIRM / WAIT, FARM-90K): Jupiter's liquidity figure; ONE price request for up to 50 held coins
    every 5 s = 720 credits an hour (use was about 26–27K an hour of the plan's ≈34.7K) and 12 requests a minute (about 385 of 600 used).
    A coin Jupiter leaves out keeps its last figure and counts a miss ("Jupiter has stopped giving a figure") — never zero.
- **Told him plainly what it can and cannot do:** a full pull happens in ONE transaction — the figure and the price fall in the same
  second, so the column cannot warn before a one-shot pull; by then the sale may have no route (Democat). It helps where liquidity is
  taken out in steps, where it is thin against the cap, and where Jupiter stops pricing the coin.
  Not known until it runs: whether Jupiter's price call gives a liquidity figure for these very new, tiny coins — check `liquidity`
  (asks / read / missed) in the new-coin status after the paste and tell him.
- Not built, only mentioned: a paper rule that sells by itself when liquidity falls a set amount (faster than a hand).
